<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that there are infinitely many primes <i>p</i> for which $`p-1`$ is squarefree and has an even number of prime factors. Equivalently, there are infinitely many primes <i>p</i> with $`\mu(p-1)=1`$.

- **claim-main-theorem** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): There are infinitely many primes $p$ such that $p-1$ is squarefree and $\Omega(p-1)$ is even. In particular, there are infinitely many such primes under either convention for counting prime factors.
  - Explanation: Infinitely many primes have a squarefree predecessor with an even number of prime factors, so the two counting conventions agree and the parity question for predecessors of primes is answered affirmatively.
- **claim-prime-estimates** (bound, [source](#stmt-pre-primes)): The prime number theorem holds with an error smaller than every fixed negative logarithmic power. Mertens’ estimates give $$\sum_{p\le y}\frac1p=\log\log y+O(1),\qquad \prod_{p\le y}\left(1-\frac1p\right) \sim\frac{e^{-\gamma_E}}{\log y},$$ where $\gamma_E$ is Euler’s constant. For fixed $A,C>0$, uniformly for $r\le(\log y)^C$ and $(a,r)=1$, the Siegel–Walfisz estimate is $$\#\{p\le y:p\equiv a\pmod r\} =\frac{\mathop{\mathrm{Li}}(y)}{\varphi(r)} +O_{A,C}\left(\frac{y}{(\log y)^A}\right).$$ Its constants need not be effective.
  - Explanation: Classical inputs: the prime number theorem with a log-power saving, Mertens estimates, and the Siegel-Walfisz estimate for small moduli, the last with possibly ineffective constants.
- **claim-large-sieve-moments** (bound, [source](#stmt-pre-moments)): For arbitrary coefficients supported on the integers of an interval of length $H$, $$\sum_{r\le R}\frac r{\varphi(r)} \sum_{\chi\bmod r}^{*} \left\|\sum_n a_n\chi(n)\right\|^2 \ll (H+1+R^2)\sum_n\|a_n\|^2.$$ The asterisk denotes primitive characters. For every fixed integer $k\ge0$ there is $C_k$ such that $$\sum_{n\le Y}\tau(n)^k\ll_k Y(\log(2Y))^{C_k},\qquad \sum_{n\le Y}\frac{\tau(n)^k}{n} \ll_k(\log(2Y))^{C_k}.$$ If $P(t)=\sum_{n\le Y}c_n n^{it}$, then on any real interval $I$ of length $H$, $$\int_I\|P(t)\|^2\,dt \ll (H+Y\log(2Y))\sum_n\|c_n\|^2.$$ For a set $\mathcal T\subset I$ with mutual spacing at least one, $$\sum_{t\in\mathcal T}\|P(t)\|^2 \ll (H+1+Y\log(2Y))(\log(2Y))^2\sum_n\|c_n\|^2.$$ The constants in the last two estimates are absolute, independently of how the coefficients were formed.
  - Explanation: Large sieve for primitive characters, divisor-moment bounds, and mean-square bounds for Dirichlet polynomials, with constants independent of how the coefficients were formed.
- **claim-derivative-tests** (bound, [source](#stmt-pre-derivatives)): Let $f$ be real-valued on an interval containing $H$ consecutive integers. If $f'$ is monotone and remains in $[j+\lambda,j+1-\lambda]$ for an integer $j$ and $0<\lambda\le1/2$, then $$\sum_n\mathrm{e}(f(n))\ll\lambda^{-1}.$$ If instead $f$ is twice continuously differentiable and $\lambda\le\|f''\|\le C\lambda$ throughout the interval, then $$\sum_n\mathrm{e}(f(n))\ll_C H\sqrt\lambda+\lambda^{-1/2}.$$ Both estimates apply on every subinterval satisfying their hypotheses.
  - Explanation: First and second derivative tests for exponential sums over consecutive integers, where the distance of the derivative from integers is part of the hypothesis.
- **claim-smooth-separation** (bound, [source](#stmt-pre-separation)): Let $F$ be smooth and supported on a fixed bounded box in $\mathbb{R}^b$, with $b$ fixed. Suppose all derivatives of order $j$ are bounded by $C_jL^{C(j+1)}$. Fourier inversion separates its variables with integrated absolute coefficient mass $L^{O(1)}$. There is a fixed exponent $C'>0$, depending on $b,C$, such that truncating each frequency at $L^{C'}$ makes an error $O_A(L^{-A})$ for every fixed $A>0$. The exponent $C'$ is fixed before $A$.
  - Explanation: A smooth function on a fixed-dimensional box separates into one-dimensional phases with log-power coefficient mass, and truncating frequencies at a fixed log power costs an arbitrarily small log saving.
- **claim-long-prime-polynomial** (bound, [source](#stmt-lp-prime-polynomial)): Fix $0<\tau<\eta<1$, $C>0$, and $A>0$. There is a constant $B_0=B_0(\tau,\eta,C,A)$ such that, uniformly for $$\frac{x^\tau}{2}\le N\le x^\eta,\qquad q\le L^C,\qquad L^{B_0}\le \|t\|\le x^2,$$ every Dirichlet character $\chi$ modulo $q$ and every interval $I\subset[N,2N]$ satisfy $$\label{lp:prime-estimate} \left\|\sum_{p\in I}\chi(p)p^{-1+it}\right\| \ll_{\tau,\eta,C,A} L^{-A}.$$
  - Explanation: A prime Dirichlet polynomial of positive-power length has arbitrary fixed log-power cancellation at all heights from a large log power up to the square of the main parameter.
- **claim-power-sum-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-lp-mean-value)): There are absolute constants $C_1,C_2>0$ such that, for every integer $k\ge2$, some integer $s$ with $k\le s\le C_1k^4$ satisfies $$\label{lp:mean-value-bound} J_{s,k}(M)\le \exp(C_1k^{C_2})M^{2s-K+1/100} \qquad(M\ge1).$$
  - Explanation: A degree-uniform Vinogradov-type mean-value bound for power-sum systems, proved by an iterative p-adic argument with explicit growth in the degree.
- **claim-log-phase** (bound, [source](#stmt-lp-log-phase)): Fix $C>0$. There is an absolute constant $C_3>0$ such that, for sufficiently large $x$ in terms of $C$, the following holds uniformly: $$\exp(L/T^2)\le N'\le2x^5,\qquad q\le L^C,\qquad \exp(L/(2T^2))\le \|v\|\le4x^3.$$ For every residue $a\pmod q$ and every interval $I\subset[N',2N']$, $$\label{lp:progression-estimate} \left\|\sum_{\substack{n\in I\\n\equiv a\pmod q}}n^{iv}\right\| \ll \frac{N'}q\exp(-L/T^{C_3}).$$
  - Explanation: A logarithmic phase sum over an arithmetic progression has a saving of exponential size in a power of the logarithm, obtained by shift averaging and a box-occupancy count.
- **claim-l-function-upper** (bound, [source](#stmt-lp-l-upper)): Fix $C>0$ and put $r_*=T^4/L$. Uniformly for $q\le L^C$, $$\label{lp:L-upper-bound} \log\|D(\sigma+iv,\chi)\|\ll_C T^2 \quad\left(\|\sigma-1\|\le10r_*,\quad \frac12\le\|v\|\le3x^3\right).$$
  - Explanation: The logarithm of a Dirichlet L-function near the line of real part one is bounded by a power of the double logarithm.
- **claim-local-log-derivative** (bound, [source](#stmt-lp-local-log-derivative)): Fix $C>0$, let $q\le L^C$, and put $s_0=1+r_*+iv$, where $1\le\|v\|\le\tfrac52x^3$. There is a radius $R$ with $4r_*\le R\le5r_*$, with no zero on its boundary, for which $$\label{lp:local-log-derivative-formula} \frac{D'}{D}(s,\chi) =\sum_{\|\rho-s_0\|<R}\frac1{s-\rho} +O_C(T^2/r_*)\qquad(\|s-s_0\|\le2r_*),$$ away from zeros. The sum counts zeros with multiplicity and has $O_C(T^2)$ terms.
  - Explanation: Near a point at large height the logarithmic derivative equals a sum over nearby zeros plus a controlled error, with few zeros in the disk.
- **claim-zero-free-strip** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lp-zero-free)): For each fixed $C>0$ there is $c=c(C)>0$ such that every character of modulus at most $L^C$ has no zero in $$\label{lp:zero-free-region} \operatorname{Re}s\ge1-cT^2/L,\qquad 1\le\|\operatorname{Im}s\|\le x^3.$$ Moreover, $$\label{lp:log-derivative-bound} \left\|\frac{D'}D(s,\chi)\right\|\ll_C L \quad\left(1-\frac{cT^2}{2L}\le\operatorname{Re}s\le1+\frac1L, \quad2\le\|\operatorname{Im}s\|\le\frac{x^3}{2}\right).$$
  - Explanation: A weak zero-free strip at large height for characters of log-power modulus, together with a bound on the logarithmic derivative inside a slightly smaller strip.
- **claim-twisted-correlation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sh-correlation)): Let $F,G$ have the form <a href="#sh:core" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="sh:core">[sh:core]</a>, with possibly different coefficients, characters, intervals, and frequencies, subject to fixed bounds as above. For every fixed $D_0>0$ there is a fixed $B>0$ with the following property. Suppose that the sequence $\alpha$ in $F$ satisfies $$\label{sh:discrepancy} \left\|\sum_{m\in I}\alpha_m\chi(m)m^{-1+it}\right\|\leq L^{-B} \quad\left( \begin{array}{l} I\subset[1,x^2]\text{ an interval},\\ \operatorname{mod}(\chi)\leq L^B,\quad \|t\|\leq L^B \end{array}\right).$$ There is no discrepancy requirement on $G$. Uniformly for $xL^{-C}\leq Z\leq xL^C$, integers $0<\|k\|\leq L^C$, $\|a_1\|,\|a_2\|\leq L^C$, and smooth functions $\psi$ supported in a fixed compact subinterval of $(0,\infty)$ with $\\|\psi^{(j)}\\|_\infty\ll_j L^{C(j+1)}$, one has $$\label{sh:correlation-bound} \sum_{\substack{z>0\\z+k>0}}\frac{\psi(z/Z)}z z^{ia_1}(z+k)^{ia_2}\overline{F(z)}G(z+k) W_\ell(z)W_\ell(z+k)\varepsilon(z)\varepsilon(z+k) \ll L^{-D_0}.$$ The exponent $B$ depends only on $D_0$ and the displayed fixed data.
  - Explanation: A shifted correlation with a prime in the long slot and the same group-supported sign at both endpoints has arbitrary log-power saving once the first sequence has small discrepancy.
- **claim-transference-input** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-sh-transference)): The following are the Local Transference Theorem, its Endpoint Pairing Corollary, and the Absolute Physical Bounds Lemma of [[SmoothShifted](#ref-SmoothShifted), Section 3, Theorem 3.5, Corollary 3.11, and Lemma 3.4]. Assume $\sum_\nu\sup\|K_\nu\|\leq L^{C_1}$, where $m_0,C_1$ are fixed independently of $J$. For every fixed $E>0$ there is an $E_{\rm id}>0$, depending only on $E$ and the group data, such that $$\sup_{\Theta\in\mathbb R} \\|\mathcal T_\zeta(\Theta)\\|_{2\to2}\leq L^{-E_{\rm id}}$$ implies the following conclusion for all sufficiently large fixed $J$. Let $I=[X,2X)$ with $x^\gamma\leq X\leq x^{1/\gamma}$ for some fixed $\gamma>0$. If $f$ is supported on positions in $I$, is independent of the chosen marks, and $$\sup\|f\|\leq\exp(O(\sqrt L)),\qquad \\|f\\|,\\|g\\|\leq X^{1/2}L^{C_3},$$ then $$\label{sh:pairing} \|\langle f,\mathcal A_\zeta g\rangle\| \ll XL^{-E+2C_3}.$$ The lower bound for $J$ may depend on $m_0,C_1$; the threshold for $x$ may also depend on $J$, the fixed exponent bounding $\|k\|$, and $\gamma$. There is no additional restriction on $\zeta$ beyond the ideal norm hypothesis. The operator obtained by taking absolute values of all transition coefficients has row and column sums at most $L^{C_{\rm abs}}$, with $C_{\rm abs}$ independent of $J$.
  - Explanation: Imported transference: a small ideal-operator norm implies a small physical pairing for admissible endpoint functions; the physical operator has controlled absolute row and column sums.
- **claim-ideal-family** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sh-ideal)): The following are the Residual Ideal Operator Theorem and Comparison Kernel Lemma of [[SmoothShifted](#ref-SmoothShifted), Section 4, Theorem 4.1 and Lemma 4.3]. For every prescribed $E_{\rm id}>0$ and fixed $C_4\geq0$, there are fixed $m_0,J_0,C_1$ such that, for each fixed $J\geq J_0$, a family consisting of the raw pattern $C_g=\varnothing$, $K_0=1$, and signed comparison patterns satisfies $$\label{sh:ideal-bound} \sum_\nu\sup\|K_\nu\|\leq L^{C_1},\qquad \sup_{\substack{\Theta\in\mathbb R\\\|\zeta\|\leq L^{C_4}}} \\|\mathcal T_\zeta(\Theta)\\|_{2\to2} \leq L^{-E_{\rm id}}.$$ The family is independent of the individual values of $\Theta,\zeta$, and all its defining parameters are independent of $J$.* *Split the big groups into two blocks. Every comparison frees nonempty sets of probes $A,B$ in the respective blocks and has coefficient $$\label{sh:comparison-kernel-product} -(-1)^{\|A\|+\|B\|}\mathcal K_A(D_A,X_A)\mathcal K_B(D_B,X_B).$$ Here $D_A,D_B$ are the free products, $X_A$ is the corresponding target-mark product, and $X_B$ the corresponding source-mark product. No shared label or final $\ell$ mark enters these kernels. For a slot set $\Lambda$, their exact form is $$\label{sh:kernel} \mathcal K_\Lambda(D,X) =\sum_{j:\nu_{\Lambda j}\geq\xi} \frac{\mathbf{1}_{\log D\in I_j}\mathbf{1}_{\log X\in I_j}}{\nu_{\Lambda j}} \sum_{\substack{\chi\ {\rm primitive}\\ \operatorname{cond}(\chi)\leq Q}} \overline{\chi(D)}\chi(X), \quad I_j=[jh,(j+1)h),$$ where $\nu_{\Lambda j}$ is the probability of the indicated cell under the independent slot laws. The quantities $Q,h^{-1},\xi^{-1}$ are fixed powers of $L$, with $h\leq1$. There are at most $Q^2$ characters and $O_{m_0}(1+TL^d/h)$ nonempty cells, and $$\sup\|\mathcal K_\Lambda\|\leq Q^2\xi^{-1},\qquad \sup_X\mathbb E_D\|\mathcal K_\Lambda(D,X)\|\leq Q^2.$$ More precisely, the parameters can be chosen, independently of $J$, so that for any fixed $A_0>0$, any tuple tests $f(X_B),g(X_A)$, and every $\theta\in\mathbb R$, $\|\zeta\|\leq L^{C_4}$, $$\begin{aligned} \Big\|\mathbb E\,\overline{f(X_B)}g(X_A) \big[&(X_AX_B)^{i\zeta}\mathrm{e}(\theta X_AX_B)\notag\\ &-\mathcal K_A(D_A,X_A)\mathcal K_B(D_B,X_B) (D_AD_B)^{i\zeta}\mathrm{e}(\theta D_AD_B)\big]\Big\| \leq L^{-A_0}\\|f\\|_2\\|g\\|_2. \label{sh:comparison-contract} \end{aligned}$$ All four label tuples in this expectation are independent. The tests need not be functions only of their products. Expanding the two kernels in <a href="#sh:comparison-kernel-product" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="sh:comparison-kernel-product">[sh:comparison-kernel-product]</a>, including all patterns, has total coefficient mass and number of terms bounded by fixed powers of $L$.
  - Explanation: Imported existence of a residual ideal family with bounded coefficient mass and small operator norm, and of comparison kernels decoupling the independent free products.
- **claim-mellin-energy** (bound, [source](#stmt-sh-mellin-energy)): Let $a_w$ be supported on $w\asymp Y$, where $Y=x^{1+o(1)}$, and let $H=x^{o(1)}$ tend to infinity. For every fixed $j\geq1$, $$\label{sh:mellin-inequality} \int_{\|\beta\|\leq1/H}\|\widehat a(\beta)\|^2\,d\beta \ll_j \frac1Y\int_{\mathbb R}(1+H\|t\|/Y)^{-j} \left\|\sum_w a_ww^{it}\right\|^2dt +\left(\frac HY\right)^2\sum_w\|a_w\|^2.$$
  - Explanation: Local Fourier energy of a sequence near zero frequency is bounded by a weighted mean square of its Mellin-type Dirichlet polynomial plus a small term.
- **claim-group-tail** (bound, [source](#stmt-wt-tail)): There is a fixed $C$ such that, for each fixed $\sigma>0$, $$\sum_{\substack{r>x^\sigma\\r_*=1}}\frac{W_1(r)}r \le L^C\exp(-\sigma L^{3/5})$$ for all sufficiently large $x$.
  - Explanation: The harmonic mass of integers supported on the group primes and exceeding a fixed power of the main parameter is smaller than any fixed log power.
- **claim-candidate-mass** (bound, [source](#stmt-wt-mass)): There is a fixed $C_A$ such that $$X_A\asymp\frac{x}{L}H_QH_P, \qquad X_A\gg xL^{-C_A}.$$ The implied constants and $C_A$ depend only on the fixed geometry and on $\Psi$.
  - Explanation: The total mass of the candidate weights is comparable to the product of the harmonic masses over a log factor and is at least a fixed negative log power times the main parameter.
- **claim-squarefree-loss** (bound, [source](#stmt-wt-squarefree)): The mass of nonsquarefree predecessors satisfies $$\sum_{\substack{u\ge1\\2u\ {\rm not\ squarefree}}} A(u)\Psi(u/x)=o(X_A/L).$$
  - Explanation: Nonsquarefree predecessors carry negligible mass compared with the mass of detected primes.
- **claim-type-ii-distribution** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ti-distribution)): Fix $D_*,b_*,C>0$. Let $U,V$ be dyadic scales such that $$UV\asymp x,\qquad x^{b_*}\le U,V\le x^{1-b_*},$$ and let $K\subset[U,2U)$ and $J\subset[V,2V)$ be intervals. Suppose $\|\alpha_m\|,\|\beta_n\|\le L^C$ and $\alpha_m=0$ unless $P^-(m)>W$. There is a fixed $B$, depending only on these fixed data and on the fixed weight and cutoff data, for which the following holds. Assume that the sequence $\alpha_m\mathbf{1}_{m\in K}$ satisfies <a href="#sh:discrepancy" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="sh:discrepancy">[sh:discrepancy]</a>; explicitly, assume $$\label{ti:actual-discrepancy} \left\|\sum_{m\in I}\alpha_m\mathbf{1}_{m\in K} \chi(m)m^{-1+it}\right\|\le L^{-B} \quad\left( \begin{array}{c} I\subset[1,x^2]\text{ an interval},\\ \operatorname{mod}(\chi)\le L^B,\quad \|t\|\le L^B \end{array}\right).$$ Then $$\label{ti:bound} \sum_{\substack{m\in K,\ n\in J,\ u\ge1\\mn=2u+1}} \alpha_m\beta_n A(u)\Psi(u/x) \ll xL^{-D_*}.$$ The estimate is uniform in the intervals and coefficient sequences satisfying these assumptions. No discrepancy or smoothness assumption is imposed on $\beta$.
  - Explanation: Bilinear sums of a rough sequence with small discrepancy against an arbitrary bounded sequence over the parity-selecting weight have arbitrary log-power saving; the proof uses a smooth allocation, Cauchy-Schwarz, a determinant change of variables and the twisted correlation theorem.
- **claim-congruence-distribution** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cg-distribution)): For every fixed $0<\vartheta<1/2$ and $D>0$, $$\sum_{\substack{d\le x^\vartheta\\d\ {\rm odd\ and\ squarefree}}} \|r(d)\|\ll_{\vartheta,D}xL^{-D}+X_A L^{-18}.$$ The constants may depend on the fixed weight geometry and $\Psi$, but not on any later proxy precision. No effective bound on the threshold for $x$ is asserted.
  - Explanation: The weight is well distributed in residue classes to odd squarefree moduli up to a fixed power below one half, via the prime slot, Siegel-Walfisz and the multiplicative large sieve.
- **claim-block-sieve** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-ex-block-sieve)): Consider finitely many objects with nonnegative weights and, at some primes $p\le z$, designated bad conditions. Suppose that for every squarefree product $d$ of these primes, including $d=1$, the weight on which all conditions indexed by $p\mid d$ hold is $Xg(d)+r(d)$. Here $X\ge0$, $g$ is multiplicative, and, for fixed $\eta_0>0$ and $C$, one has $$0\le g(p)\le1-\eta_0,\qquad \sum_{w<p\le w^2}g(p)\le C\quad(w>1).$$ For every sufficiently large even integer $h$, the weight avoiding all the bad conditions equals $$\label{ex:block-formula} X\prod_{p\le z}(1-g(p))(1+O(e^{-h})) +O\!\left(\sum_{\substack{d\le z^{4h+2}\\d\text{ squarefree}}} \|r(d)\|\right).$$ The products and sums use only the designated primes. Constants depend only on the two density bounds, and are uniform when $h$ grows. For $h=2$ there is an upper bound by a fixed constant times the displayed main term, plus the same remainder sum.* *The upper and lower inclusion–exclusion polynomials have coefficients of absolute value at most one, supported on squarefree $d\le z^{4h+2}$. The upper polynomial is nonnegative. Its overcount is bounded by the nonnegative difference between the upper and lower polynomials; that difference also has coefficients of absolute value at most one and the same support bound.
  - Explanation: Imported weighted block sieve with growing depth, giving asymptotics for the weight avoiding all bad conditions and a fixed-depth upper bound, with polynomial coefficients bounded by one.
- **claim-proxy-estimates** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-ex-proxies)): For sufficiently large $x$, the denominators in <a href="#ex:proxy-definition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ex:proxy-definition">[ex:proxy-definition]</a> are positive and $0\le c_I\le1$. For every fixed $B>0$, every fixed $S_1>2B+3$, and every interval $K$ inside a dyad in the stated factor ranges, the sequence $$\alpha_m=(I(m)-B_I(m))\mathbf{1}_{m\in K}$$ vanishes outside $P^-(m)>W$, has absolute value at most one, and satisfies $$\label{ex:proxy-discrepancy} \left\|\sum_{m\in J}\alpha_m\chi(m)m^{-1+it}\right\|\le L^{-B}$$ for every interval $J\subset[1,x^2]$, every Dirichlet character of modulus at most $L^B$, and every $\|t\|\le L^B$.* *Write $$\mathcal B_\kappa=[x^{1/2-2\kappa},x^{1/2+2\kappa}].$$ There is an absolute constant $C_{\mathrm{pr}}$ such that $$\label{ex:prime-density} c_{\mathrm{pr}}(m)\le\frac{C_{\mathrm{pr}}}{LV(W)} \qquad(m\in\mathcal B_\kappa).$$ The constant is independent of $\kappa$ and $S_1$; the threshold for $x$ may depend on all fixed parameters.* *For $w>\gamma>0$, set $$\label{ex:rough-density} D_\gamma(w)=\frac1w+ \sum_{l\ge2}\frac1{l!} \int_{\substack{t_i\ge\gamma\ (1\le i<l)\\ \sum_{i<l}t_i\le w-\gamma}} \frac{\prod_{i<l}(dt_i/t_i)}{w-\sum_{i<l}t_i}.$$ This sum is locally finite and nonnegative, and is jointly continuous on compact subsets of $w>\gamma>0$, including the thresholds $w=l\gamma$. If $mn=N_u$ on the support of $\Psi(u/x)$ and $\log n/L\in(\gamma,\gamma']$, then $$\label{ex:rough-proxy-bound} c_\gamma(m)\le \frac{\sup_{\gamma\le t\le\gamma'}D_\gamma(1-t)+o(1)}{LV(W)}.$$ Here the parameters are restricted to a compact subset of $w>\gamma>0$, as holds for the bins under consideration. For fixed sufficiently small $b>0$, $$\begin{aligned} \int_b^{1/2}D_\alpha(1-\alpha)\frac{d\alpha}{\alpha} &=D_b(1)-1,\label{ex:density-integral}\\ D_b(1)&\le\frac{e^{-\gamma_E}}b(1+O(e^{-c/b})) \label{ex:density-upper} \end{aligned}$$ with an absolute $c>0$. The endpoint of the integral is interpreted by its left limit.
  - Explanation: Imported local-density proxies for primality and roughness: bounded coefficients, small discrepancy against characters and phases, an absolute prime-density bound and a rough-density upper bound.
- **claim-assignment-mask-majorant** (bound, [source](#stmt-ex-mask)): An assignment $a$ consists of the complete ordered $r_0$-tuples in all the micro bands and one prime mark from each $\mathcal P_g$. Let $D_1=D_1(a)$ be their product, with multiplicities retained, and put $$\lambda_a=\prod_{j=j'}^{j_x}\frac1{r_0!}\prod_g V_g^{-1}.$$ For sufficiently large $x$, $$\label{ex:assignment-masses} D_1\le x^{1/20},\qquad \sum_a\frac{\lambda_a}{D_1}\ll H_Q,\qquad \sum_a\lambda_a\ll x^{1/20}H_Q.$$ For each assignment, ban every odd non-$\mathcal P$ prime at most $x^\theta$ not dividing $D_1$, and independently ban each $\mathcal P$ prime not dividing $D_1$ with probability $1-q_0$. With expectation over this finite random mask, $$\label{ex:positive-majorant} A(u)\le A_0(u)\le \sum_a\lambda_a\mathbf{1}_{D_1\mid u}\,\mathbb E_a \mathbf{1}_{u\text{ is divisible by no banned prime}}.$$ The constants in <a href="#ex:assignment-masses" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ex:assignment-masses">[ex:assignment-masses]</a> depend only on the fixed weight geometry and $j'$.
  - Explanation: The weight is dominated by a sum over assignments of micro-band tuples and group marks, with a random mask banning small primes, and the assignment masses are controlled.
- **claim-positive-dyadic-box** (bound, [source](#stmt-ex-box)): There is a constant $C_{\mathrm{box}}$, depending only on the fixed weight data and the choices in <a href="#ex:micro-choice" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ex:micro-choice">[ex:micro-choice]</a>, such that, on every dyadic box with $M_1M_2\asymp x$ and $M_i\ll x^{0.54}$, $$\label{ex:box-bound} \sum_{\substack{M_1\le m<2M_1,\ M_2\le n<2M_2\\ mn=N_u,\ P^-(m),P^-(n)>W}} a_x(u)\le C_{\mathrm{box}}X_AV(W)^2.$$ The fixed constants in the size comparisons can be taken to cover all boxes meeting $mn=2u+1$ with $u\in[x,2x]$.
  - Explanation: On each dyadic box of moderate size the weight on rough factor pairs is at most a constant times the total mass times the squared Mertens product, by the fixed-depth block sieve.
- **claim-test-replacement** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ex-replacement)): In all factor sums used below, a test $I$ on either factor of $mn=N_u$ can be replaced by $B_I$ with total error $o(X_A/L)$, provided the coefficient on the other factor has absolute value at most one. This holds also when that other coefficient is a proxy.
  - Explanation: Prime and roughness tests on either factor can be replaced by their local-density proxies at a cost that is negligible against the mass over the log factor.
- **claim-initial-sifted-mass** (bound, [source](#stmt-ex-presieve)): For sufficiently small fixed $b_1$, $$\label{ex:presieve-bound} \sum_{P^-(N_u)>x^{b_1}}a_x(u) \ge\frac{\mathfrak S X_A}{L} \left\{\frac{e^{-\gamma_E}}{b_1} (1-O(e^{-c/b_1}))+o(1)\right\}.$$
  - Explanation: The weight of values with no small prime factor has an explicit lower bound from the block sieve and the congruence distribution theorem.
- **claim-conditional-sieve** (bound, [source](#stmt-ex-conditional)): For each fixed bin $(\gamma,\gamma']$ of the chosen partition, $$\label{ex:conditional-sieve} \sum_{\substack{x^\gamma<n\le x^{\gamma'}\\n\text{ prime}}} \sum_{\substack{n\mid N_u\\P^-(N_u)>W}}a_x(u) =X_AV_1(W)\{\log(\gamma'/\gamma)+o(1)\}.$$
  - Explanation: The weight of values divisible by a prime in a fixed bin, after sifting by small primes, equals the expected main term, using injectivity of the product map.
- **claim-unbalanced-removal** (bound, [source](#stmt-ex-unbalanced)): The weight of prime values $N_u$, together with composite values whose least prime factor exceeds $x^{1/2-\kappa}$, is at least $$\label{ex:surviving-mass} \frac{\mathfrak S X_A}{2L}$$ for sufficiently large $x$ after $b_1$ is chosen sufficiently small.
  - Explanation: After subtracting the unbalanced composites through the rough-density integral, at least half of the presieved mass remains on primes and on composites with large least prime factor.
- **claim-balanced-composites** (bound, [source](#stmt-ex-balanced)): With the constant fixed in <a href="#ex:balanced-constant" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ex:balanced-constant">[ex:balanced-constant]</a>, the weight of composite $N_u$ with $P^-(N_u)>x^{1/2-\kappa}$ is at most $$\label{ex:balanced-bound} C_{\mathrm{bal}}(\kappa+1/L)\frac{X_A}{L}+o(X_A/L).$$
  - Explanation: Composites with two prime factors in the balanced band have weight bounded by a constant times a small parameter times the mass over the log factor, via the proxy replacement and the dyadic box bound.

```json
        "exact_quote": "Let $f$ be real-valued on an interval containing $H$ consecutive integers.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-sh-correlation",
        "exact_quote": "Let $F,G$ have the form <a href=\"#sh:core\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"sh:core\">[sh:core]</a>, with possibly different coefficients, characters, intervals, and frequencies, subject to fixed bounds as above. For every fixed $D_0>0$ there is a fixed $B>0$ with the following property. Suppose that the sequence $\\alpha$ in $F$ satisfies $$\\label{sh:discrepancy}\n \\left|\\sum_{m\\in I}\\alpha_m\\chi(m)m^{-1+it}\\right|\\leq L^{-B}\n \\quad\\left(\n \\begin{array}{l}\n I\\subset[1,x^2]\\text{ an interval},\\\\\n \\operatorname{mod}(\\chi)\\leq L^B,\\quad |t|\\leq L^B\n \\end{array}\\right).$$ There is no discrepancy requirement on $G$. Uniformly for $xL^{-C}\\leq Z\\leq xL^C$, integers $0<|k|\\leq L^C$, $|a_1|,|a_2|\\leq L^C$, and smooth functions $\\psi$ supported in a fixed compact subinterval of $(0,\\infty)$ with $\\|\\psi^{(j)}\\|_\\infty\\ll_j L^{C(j+1)}$, one has $$\\label{sh:correlation-bound}\n \\sum_{\\substack{z>0\\\\z+k>0}}\\frac{\\psi(z/Z)}z\n z^{ia_1}(z+k)^{ia_2}\\overline{F(z)}G(z+k)\n W_\\ell(z)W_\\ell(z+k)\\varepsilon(z)\\varepsilon(z+k)\n \\ll L^{-D_0}.$$ The exponent $B$ depends only on $D_0$ and the displayed fixed data.",
        "source_id": "PM-011.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-sh-correlation",
          "exact_quote": "Let $F,G$ have the form <a href=\"#sh:core\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"sh:core\">[sh:core]</a>, with possibly different coefficients, characters, intervals, and frequencies, subject to fixed bounds as above. For every fixed $D_0>0$ there is a fixed $B>0$ with the following property. Suppose that the sequence $\\alpha$ in $F$ satisfies $$\\label{sh:discrepancy}\n \\left|\\sum_{m\\in I}\\alpha_m\\chi(m)m^{-1+it}\\right|\\leq L^{-B}\n \\quad\\left(\n \\begin{array}{l}\n I\\subset[1,x^2]\\text{ an interval},\\\\\n \\operatorname{mod}(\\chi)\\leq L^B,\\quad |t|\\leq L^B\n \\end{array}\\right).$$ There is no discrepancy requirement on $G$. Uniformly for $xL^{-C}\\leq Z\\leq xL^C$, integers $0<|k|\\leq L^C$, $|a_1|,|a_2|\\leq L^C$, and smooth functions $\\psi$ supported in a fixed compact subinterval of $(0,\\infty)$ with $\\|\\psi^{(j)}\\|_\\infty\\ll_j L^{C(j+1)}$, one has $$\\label{sh:correlation-bound}\n \\sum_{\\substack{z>0\\\\z+k>0}}\\frac{\\psi(z/Z)}z\n z^{ia_1}(z+k)^{ia_2}\\overline{F(z)}G(z+k)\n W_\\ell(z)W_\\ell(z+k)\\varepsilon(z)\\varepsilon(z+k)\n \\ll L^{-D_0}.$$ The exponent $B$ depends only on $D_0$ and the displayed fixed data.",
          "source_id": "PM-011.3"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Let $F,G$ have the form <a href=\"#sh:core\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"sh:core\">[sh:core]</a>, with possibly different coefficients, characters, intervals, and frequencies, subject to fixed bounds as above. For every fixed $D_0>0$ there is a fixed $B>0$ with the following property. Suppose that the sequence $\\alpha$ in $F$ satisfies $$\\label{sh:discrepancy}\n \\left|\\sum_{m\\in I}\\alpha_m\\chi(m)m^{-1+it}\\right|\\leq L^{-B}\n \\quad\\left(\n \\begin{array}{l}\n I\\subset[1,x^2]\\text{ an interval},\\\\\n \\operatorname{mod}(\\chi)\\leq L^B,\\quad |t|\\leq L^B\n \\end{array}\\right).$$ There is no discrepancy requirement on $G$. Uniformly for $xL^{-C}\\leq Z\\leq xL^C$, integers $0<|k|\\leq L^C$, $|a_1|,|a_2|\\leq L^C$, and smooth functions $\\psi$ supported in a fixed compact subinterval of $(0,\\infty)$ with $\\|\\psi^{(j)}\\|_\\infty\\ll_j L^{C(j+1)}$, one has $$\\label{sh:correlation-bound}\n \\sum_{\\substack{z>0\\\\z+k>0}}\\frac{\\psi(z/Z)}z\n z^{ia_1}(z+k)^{ia_2}\\overline{F(z)}G(z+k)\n W_\\ell(z)W_\\ell(z+k)\\varepsilon(z)\\varepsilon(z+k)\n \\ll L^{-D_0}.$$ The exponent $B$ depends only on $D_0$ and the displayed fixed data.",
      "explanation": "A shifted correlation with a prime in the long slot and the same group-supported sign at both endpoints has arbitrary log-power saving once the first sequence has small discrepancy.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-sh-transference",
        "exact_quote": "The following are the Local Transference Theorem, its Endpoint Pairing Corollary, and the Absolute Physical Bounds Lemma of [[SmoothShifted](#ref-SmoothShifted), Section 3, Theorem 3.5, Corollary 3.11, and Lemma 3.4]. Assume $\\sum_\\nu\\sup|K_\\nu|\\leq L^{C_1}$, where $m_0,C_1$ are fixed independently of $J$. For every fixed $E>0$ there is an $E_{\\rm id}>0$, depending only on $E$ and the group data, such that $$\\sup_{\\Theta\\in\\mathbb R}\n       \\|\\mathcal T_\\zeta(\\Theta)\\|_{2\\to2}\\leq L^{-E_{\\rm id}}$$ implies the following conclusion for all sufficiently large fixed $J$. Let $I=[X,2X)$ with $x^\\gamma\\leq X\\leq x^{1/\\gamma}$ for some fixed $\\gamma>0$. If $f$ is supported on positions in $I$, is independent of the chosen marks, and $$\\sup|f|\\leq\\exp(O(\\sqrt L)),\\qquad\n \\|f\\|,\\|g\\|\\leq X^{1/2}L^{C_3},$$ then $$\\label{sh:pairing}\n |\\langle f,\\mathcal A_\\zeta g\\rangle|\n \\ll XL^{-E+2C_3}.$$ The lower bound for $J$ may depend on $m_0,C_1$; the threshold for $x$ may also depend on $J$, the fixed exponent bounding $|k|$, and $\\gamma$. There is no additional restriction on $\\zeta$ beyond the ideal norm hypothesis. The operator obtained by taking absolute values of all transition coefficients has row and column sums at most $L^{C_{\\rm abs}}$, with $C_{\\rm abs}$ independent of $J$.",
        "source_id": "PM-011.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-sh-transference",
          "exact_quote": "The following are the Local Transference Theorem, its Endpoint Pairing Corollary, and the Absolute Physical Bounds Lemma of [[SmoothShifted](#ref-SmoothShifted), Section 3, Theorem 3.5, Corollary 3.11, and Lemma 3.4]. Let $I=[X,2X)$ with $x^\\gamma\\leq X\\leq x^{1/\\gamma}$ for some fixed $\\gamma>0$. If $f$ is supported on positions in $I$, is independent of the chosen marks, and $$\\sup|f|\\leq\\exp(O(\\sqrt L)),\\qquad\n \\|f\\|,\\|g\\|\\leq X^{1/2}L^{C_3},$$ then $$\\label{sh:pairing}\n |\\langle f,\\mathcal A_\\zeta g\\rangle|\n \\ll XL^{-E+2C_3}.$$ The lower bound for $J$ may depend on $m_0,C_1$; the threshold for $x$ may also depend on $J$, the fixed exponent bounding $|k|$, and $\\gamma$.         }

      "exact_statement": "The following are the Local Transference Theorem, its Endpoint Pairing Corollary, and the Absolute Physical Bounds Lemma of [[SmoothShifted](#ref-SmoothShifted), Section 3, Theorem 3.5, Corollary 3.11, and Lemma 3.4]. Assume $\\sum_\\nu\\sup|K_\\nu|\\leq L^{C_1}$, where $m_0,C_1$ are fixed independently of $J$. For every fixed $E>0$ there is an $E_{\\rm id}>0$, depending only on $E$ and the group data, such that $$\\sup_{\\Theta\\in\\mathbb R}\n       \\|\\mathcal T_\\zeta(\\Theta)\\|_{2\\to2}\\leq L^{-E_{\\rm id}}$$ implies the following conclusion for all sufficiently large fixed $J$. Let $I=[X,2X)$ with $x^\\gamma\\leq X\\leq x^{1/\\gamma}$ for some fixed $\\gamma>0$. If $f$ is supported on positions in $I$, is independent of the chosen marks, and $$\\sup|f|\\leq\\exp(O(\\sqrt L)),\\qquad\n \\|f\\|,\\|g\\|\\leq X^{1/2}L^{C_3},$$ then $$\\label{sh:pairing}\n |\\langle f,\\mathcal A_\\zeta g\\rangle|\n \\ll XL^{-E+2C_3}.$$ The lower bound for $J$ may depend on $m_0,C_1$; the threshold for $x$ may also depend on $J$, the fixed exponent bounding $|k|$, and $\\gamma$. The operator obtained by taking absolute values of all transition coefficients has row and column sums at most $L^{C_{\\rm abs}}$, with $C_{\\rm abs}$ independent of $J$.",
      "explanation": "Imported transference: a small ideal-operator norm implies a small physical pairing for admissible endpoint functions; the physical operator has controlled absolute row and column sums.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [

      "id": "claim-transference-input",
      "kind": "conditional_result",
      "quantifiers": [

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-sh-ideal",
        "exact_quote": "The following are the Residual Ideal Operator Theorem and Comparison Kernel Lemma of [[SmoothShifted](#ref-SmoothShifted), Section 4, Theorem 4.1 and Lemma 4.3]. For every prescribed $E_{\\rm id}>0$ and fixed $C_4\\geq0$, there are fixed $m_0,J_0,C_1$ such that, for each fixed $J\\geq J_0$, a family consisting of the raw pattern $C_g=\\varnothing$, $K_0=1$, and signed comparison patterns satisfies $$\\label{sh:ideal-bound}\n \\sum_\\nu\\sup|K_\\nu|\\leq L^{C_1},\\qquad\n \\sup_{\\substack{\\Theta\\in\\mathbb R\\\\|\\zeta|\\leq L^{C_4}}}\n       \\|\\mathcal T_\\zeta(\\Theta)\\|_{2\\to2}\n       \\leq L^{-E_{\\rm id}}.$$ The family is independent of the individual values of $\\Theta,\\zeta$, and all its defining parameters are independent of $J$.*\n\n\n\n*Split the big groups into two blocks. Every comparison frees nonempty sets of probes $A,B$ in the respective blocks and has coefficient $$\\label{sh:comparison-kernel-product}\n -(-1)^{|A|+|B|}\\mathcal K_A(D_A,X_A)\\mathcal K_B(D_B,X_B).$$ Here $D_A,D_B$ are the free products, $X_A$ is the corresponding target-mark product, and $X_B$ the corresponding source-mark product. No shared label or final $\\ell$ mark enters these kernels. For a slot set $\\Lambda$, their exact form is $$\\label{sh:kernel}\n \\mathcal K_\\Lambda(D,X)\n =\\sum_{j:\\nu_{\\Lambda j}\\geq\\xi}\n   \\frac{\\mathbf{1}_{\\log D\\in I_j}\\mathbf{1}_{\\log X\\in I_j}}{\\nu_{\\Lambda j}}\n   \\sum_{\\substack{\\chi\\ {\\rm primitive}\\\\\n                    \\operatorname{cond}(\\chi)\\leq Q}}\n               \\overline{\\chi(D)}\\chi(X),\n \\quad I_j=[jh,(j+1)h),$$ where $\\nu_{\\Lambda j}$ is the probability of the indicated cell under the independent slot laws. The quantities $Q,h^{-1},\\xi^{-1}$ are fixed powers of $L$, with $h\\leq1$. There are at most $Q^2$ characters and $O_{m_0}(1+TL^d/h)$ nonempty cells, and $$\\sup|\\mathcal K_\\Lambda|\\leq Q^2\\xi^{-1},\\qquad\n \\sup_X\\mathbb E_D|\\mathcal K_\\Lambda(D,X)|\\leq Q^2.$$ More precisely, the parameters can be chosen, independently of $J$, so that for any fixed $A_0>0$, any tuple tests $f(X_B),g(X_A)$, and every $\\theta\\in\\mathbb R$, $|\\zeta|\\leq L^{C_4}$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\Big|\\mathbb E\\,\\overline{f(X_B)}g(X_A)\n \\big[&(X_AX_B)^{i\\zeta}\\mathrm{e}(\\theta X_AX_B)\\notag\\\\\n &-\\mathcal K_A(D_A,X_A)\\mathcal K_B(D_B,X_B)\n       (D_AD_B)^{i\\zeta}\\mathrm{e}(\\theta D_AD_B)\\big]\\Big|\n \\leq L^{-A_0}\\|f\\|_2\\|g\\|_2.\n \\label{sh:comparison-contract}\n\\end{aligned}$$ All four label tuples in this expectation are independent. The tests need not be functions only of their products. Expanding the two kernels in <a href=\"#sh:comparison-kernel-product\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"sh:comparison-kernel-product\">[sh:comparison-kernel-product]</a>, including all patterns, has total coefficient mass and number of terms bounded by fixed powers of $L$.",
        "source_id": "PM-011.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-sh-ideal",
          "exact_quote": "The following are the Residual Ideal Operator Theorem and Comparison Kernel Lemma of [[SmoothShifted](#ref-SmoothShifted), Section 4, Theorem 4.1 and Lemma 4.3]. For every prescribed $E_{\\rm id}>0$ and fixed $C_4\\geq0$, there are fixed $m_0,J_0,C_1$ such that, for each fixed $J\\geq J_0$, a family consisting of the raw pattern $C_g=\\varnothing$, $K_0=1$, and signed comparison patterns satisfies $$\\label{sh:ideal-bound}\n \\sum_\\nu\\sup|K_\\nu|\\leq L^{C_1},\\qquad\n \\sup_{\\substack{\\Theta\\in\\mathbb R\\\\|\\zeta|\\leq L^{C_4}}}\n       \\|\\mathcal T_\\zeta(\\Theta)\\|_{2\\to2}\n       \\leq L^{-E_{\\rm id}}.$$ The family is independent of the individual values of $\\Theta,\\zeta$, and all its defining parameters are independent of $J$.*\n\n\n\n*Split the big groups into two blocks. Every comparison frees nonempty sets of probes $A,B$ in the respective blocks and has coefficient $$\\label{sh:comparison-kernel-product}\n -(-1)^{|A|+|B|}\\mathcal K_A(D_A,X_A)\\mathcal K_B(D_B,X_B).$$ Here $D_A,D_B$ are the free products, $X_A$ is the corresponding target-mark product, and $X_B$ the corresponding source-mark product. No shared label or final $\\ell$ mark enters these kernels. For a slot set $\\Lambda$, their exact form is $$\\label{sh:kernel}\n \\mathcal K_\\Lambda(D,X)\n =\\sum_{j:\\nu_{\\Lambda j}\\geq\\xi}\n   \\frac{\\mathbf{1}_{\\log D\\in I_j}\\mathbf{1}_{\\log X\\in I_j}}{\\nu_{\\Lambda j}}\n   \\sum_{\\substack{\\chi\\ {\\rm primitive}\\\\\n                    \\operatorname{cond}(\\chi)\\leq Q}}\n               \\overline{\\chi(D)}\\chi(X),\n \\quad I_j=[jh,(j+1)h),$$ where $\\nu_{\\Lambda j}$ is the probability of the indicated cell under the independent slot laws. The quantities $Q,h^{-1},\\xi^{-1}$ are fixed powers of $L$, with $h\\leq1$. There are at most $Q^2$ characters and $O_{m_0}(1+TL^d/h)$ nonempty cells, and $$\\sup|\\mathcal K_\\Lambda|\\leq Q^2\\xi^{-1},\\qquad\n \\sup_X\\mathbb E_D|\\mathcal K_\\Lambda(D,X)|\\leq Q^2.$$ More precisely, the parameters can be chosen, independently of $J$, so that for any fixed $A_0>0$, any tuple tests $f(X_B),g(X_A)$, and every $\\theta\\in\\mathbb R$, $|\\zeta|\\leq L^{C_4}$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\Big|\\mathbb E\\,\\overline{f(X_B)}g(X_A)\n \\big[&(X_AX_B)^{i\\zeta}\\mathrm{e}(\\theta X_AX_B)\\notag\\\\\n &-\\mathcal K_A(D_A,X_A)\\mathcal K_B(D_B,X_B)\n       (D_AD_B)^{i\\zeta}\\mathrm{e}(\\theta D_AD_B)\\big]\\Big|\n \\leq L^{-A_0}\\|f\\|_2\\|g\\|_2.\n \\label{sh:comparison-contract}\n\\end{aligned}$$ All four label tuples in this expectation are independent. The tests need not be functions only of their products. Expanding the two kernels in <a href=\"#sh:comparison-kernel-product\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"sh:comparison-kernel-product\">[sh:comparison-kernel-product]</a>, including all patterns, has total coefficient mass and number of terms bounded by fixed powers of $L$.",
          "source_id": "PM-011.3"
        }

      "exact_statement": "The following are the Residual Ideal Operator Theorem and Comparison Kernel Lemma of [[SmoothShifted](#ref-SmoothShifted), Section 4, Theorem 4.1 and Lemma 4.3]. Every comparison frees nonempty sets of probes $A,B$ in the respective blocks and has coefficient $$\\label{sh:comparison-kernel-product}\n -(-1)^{|A|+|B|}\\mathcal K_A(D_A,X_A)\\mathcal K_B(D_B,X_B).$$ Here $D_A,D_B$ are the free products, $X_A$ is the corresponding target-mark product, and $X_B$ the corresponding source-mark product. The tests need not be functions only of their products. Expanding the two kernels in <a href=\"#sh:comparison-kernel-product\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"sh:comparison-kernel-product\">[sh:comparison-kernel-product]</a>, including all patterns, has total coefficient mass and number of terms bounded by fixed powers of $L$.",
      "explanation": "Imported existence of a residual ideal family with bounded coefficient mass and small operator norm, and of comparison kernels decoupling the independent free products.",

      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-sh-ideal",
          "exact_quote": "The following are the Residual Ideal Operator Theorem and Comparison Kernel Lemma of [[SmoothShifted](#ref-SmoothShifted), Section 4, Theorem 4.1 and Lemma 4.3]. For every prescribed $E_{\\rm id}>0$ and fixed $C_4\\geq0$, there are fixed $m_0,J_0,C_1$ such that, for each fixed $J\\geq J_0$, a family consisting of the raw pattern $C_g=\\varnothing$, $K_0=1$, and signed comparison patterns satisfies $$\\label{sh:ideal-bound}\n \\sum_\\nu\\sup|K_\\nu|\\leq L^{C_1},\\qquad\n \\sup_{\\substack{\\Theta\\in\\mathbb R\\\\|\\zeta|\\leq L^{C_4}}}\n       \\|\\mathcal T_\\zeta(\\Theta)\\|_{2\\to2}\n       \\leq L^{-E_{\\rm id}}.$$ The family is independent of the individual values of $\\Theta,\\zeta$, and all its defining parameters are independent of $J$.*\n\n\n\n*Split the big groups into two blocks.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-sh-mellin-energy",
        "exact_quote": "Let $a_w$ be supported on $w\\asymp Y$, where $Y=x^{1+o(1)}$, and let $H=x^{o(1)}$ tend to infinity. For every fixed $j\\geq1$, $$\\label{sh:mellin-inequality}\n \\int_{|\\beta|\\leq1/H}|\\widehat a(\\beta)|^2\\,d\\beta\n \\ll_j \\frac1Y\\int_{\\mathbb R}(1+H|t|/Y)^{-j}\n           \\left|\\sum_w a_ww^{it}\\right|^2dt\n       +\\left(\\frac HY\\right)^2\\sum_w|a_w|^2.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-011.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-sh-mellin-energy",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-wt-tail",
        "exact_quote": "There is a fixed $C$ such that, for each fixed $\\sigma>0$, $$\\sum_{\\substack{r>x^\\sigma\\\\r_*=1}}\\frac{W_1(r)}r\n \\le L^C\\exp(-\\sigma L^{3/5})$$ for all sufficiently large $x$.",
        "source_id": "PM-011.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

        "exact_quote": "Fix $D_*,b_*,C>0$. Let $U,V$ be dyadic scales such that $$UV\\asymp x,\\qquad x^{b_*}\\le U,V\\le x^{1-b_*},$$ and let $K\\subset[U,2U)$ and $J\\subset[V,2V)$ be intervals. Suppose $|\\alpha_m|,|\\beta_n|\\le L^C$ and $\\alpha_m=0$ unless $P^-(m)>W$. There is a fixed $B$, depending only on these fixed data and on the fixed weight and cutoff data, for which the following holds. Assume that the sequence $\\alpha_m\\mathbf{1}_{m\\in K}$ satisfies <a href=\"#sh:discrepancy\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"sh:discrepancy\">[sh:discrepancy]</a>; explicitly, assume $$\\label{ti:actual-discrepancy}\n \\left|\\sum_{m\\in I}\\alpha_m\\mathbf{1}_{m\\in K}\n                    \\chi(m)m^{-1+it}\\right|\\le L^{-B}\n \\quad\\left(\n \\begin{array}{c}\n I\\subset[1,x^2]\\text{ an interval},\\\\\n \\operatorname{mod}(\\chi)\\le L^B,\\quad |t|\\le L^B\n \\end{array}\\right).$$ Then $$\\label{ti:bound}\n \\sum_{\\substack{m\\in K,\\ n\\in J,\\ u\\ge1\\\\mn=2u+1}}\n       \\alpha_m\\beta_n A(u)\\Psi(u/x)\n       \\ll xL^{-D_*}.$$ The estimate is uniform in the intervals and coefficient sequences satisfying these assumptions.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ex-block-sieve",
        "exact_quote": "Consider finitely many objects with nonnegative weights and, at some primes $p\\le z$, designated bad conditions. Suppose that for every squarefree product $d$ of these primes, including $d=1$, the weight on which all conditions indexed by $p\\mid d$ hold is $Xg(d)+r(d)$. Here $X\\ge0$, $g$ is multiplicative, and, for fixed $\\eta_0>0$ and $C$, one has $$0\\le g(p)\\le1-\\eta_0,\\qquad\n \\sum_{w<p\\le w^2}g(p)\\le C\\quad(w>1).$$ For every sufficiently large even integer $h$, the weight avoiding all the bad conditions equals $$\\label{ex:block-formula}\n X\\prod_{p\\le z}(1-g(p))(1+O(e^{-h}))\n +O\\!\\left(\\sum_{\\substack{d\\le z^{4h+2}\\\\d\\text{ squarefree}}}\n                 |r(d)|\\right).$$ The products and sums use only the designated primes. Constants depend only on the two density bounds, and are uniform when $h$ grows. For $h=2$ there is an upper bound by a fixed constant times the displayed main term, plus the same remainder sum.*\n\n*The upper and lower inclusion–exclusion polynomials have coefficients of absolute value at most one, supported on squarefree $d\\le z^{4h+2}$. The upper polynomial is nonnegative. Its overcount is bounded by the nonnegative difference between the upper and lower polynomials; that difference also has coefficients of absolute value at most one and the same support bound.",
        "source_id": "PM-011.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-ex-block-sieve",
          "exact_quote": "Consider finitely many objects with nonnegative weights and, at some primes $p\\le z$, designated bad conditions. Constants depend only on the two density bounds, and are uniform when $h$ grows. For $h=2$ there is an upper bound by a fixed constant times the displayed main term, plus the same remainder sum.*\n\n*The upper and lower inclusion–exclusion polynomials have coefficients of absolute value at most one, supported on squarefree $d\\le z^{4h+2}$. The upper polynomial is nonnegative. Its overcount is bounded by the nonnegative difference between the upper and lower polynomials; that difference also has coefficients of absolute value at most one and the same support bound.",
          "source_id": "PM-011.3"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ex-proxies",
        "exact_quote": "For sufficiently large $x$, the denominators in <a href=\"#ex:proxy-definition\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ex:proxy-definition\">[ex:proxy-definition]</a> are positive and $0\\le c_I\\le1$. For every fixed $B>0$, every fixed $S_1>2B+3$, and every interval $K$ inside a dyad in the stated factor ranges, the sequence $$\\alpha_m=(I(m)-B_I(m))\\mathbf{1}_{m\\in K}$$ vanishes outside $P^-(m)>W$, has absolute value at most one, and satisfies $$\\label{ex:proxy-discrepancy}\n \\left|\\sum_{m\\in J}\\alpha_m\\chi(m)m^{-1+it}\\right|\\le L^{-B}$$ for every interval $J\\subset[1,x^2]$, every Dirichlet character of modulus at most $L^B$, and every $|t|\\le L^B$.*\n\n\n*Write $$\\mathcal B_\\kappa=[x^{1/2-2\\kappa},x^{1/2+2\\kappa}].$$ There is an absolute constant $C_{\\mathrm{pr}}$ such that $$\\label{ex:prime-density}\n c_{\\mathrm{pr}}(m)\\le\\frac{C_{\\mathrm{pr}}}{LV(W)}\n       \\qquad(m\\in\\mathcal B_\\kappa).$$ The constant is independent of $\\kappa$ and $S_1$; the threshold for $x$ may depend on all fixed parameters.*\n\n\n\n\n*For $w>\\gamma>0$, set $$\\label{ex:rough-density}\n D_\\gamma(w)=\\frac1w+\n \\sum_{l\\ge2}\\frac1{l!}\n \\int_{\\substack{t_i\\ge\\gamma\\ (1\\le i<l)\\\\\n                   \\sum_{i<l}t_i\\le w-\\gamma}}\n       \\frac{\\prod_{i<l}(dt_i/t_i)}{w-\\sum_{i<l}t_i}.$$ This sum is locally finite and nonnegative, and is jointly continuous on compact subsets of $w>\\gamma>0$, including the thresholds $w=l\\gamma$. If $mn=N_u$ on the support of $\\Psi(u/x)$ and $\\log n/L\\in(\\gamma,\\gamma']$, then $$\\label{ex:rough-proxy-bound}\n c_\\gamma(m)\\le\n \\frac{\\sup_{\\gamma\\le t\\le\\gamma'}D_\\gamma(1-t)+o(1)}{LV(W)}.$$ Here the parameters are restricted to a compact subset of $w>\\gamma>0$, as holds for the bins under consideration. For fixed sufficiently small $b>0$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\int_b^{1/2}D_\\alpha(1-\\alpha)\\frac{d\\alpha}{\\alpha}\n     &=D_b(1)-1,\\label{ex:density-integral}\\\\\n D_b(1)&\\le\\frac{e^{-\\gamma_E}}b(1+O(e^{-c/b}))\n       \\label{ex:density-upper}\n\\end{aligned}$$ with an absolute $c>0$. The endpoint of the integral is interpreted by its left limit.",
        "source_id": "PM-011.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-ex-proxies",
          "exact_quote": "For sufficiently large $x$, the denominators in <a href=\"#ex:proxy-definition\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ex:proxy-definition\">[ex:proxy-definition]</a> are positive and $0\\le c_I\\le1$. If $mn=N_u$ on the support of $\\Psi(u/x)$ and $\\log n/L\\in(\\gamma,\\gamma']$, then $$\\label{ex:rough-proxy-bound}\n c_\\gamma(m)\\le\n \\frac{\\sup_{\\gamma\\le t\\le\\gamma'}D_\\gamma(1-t)+o(1)}{LV(W)}.$$ Here the parameters are restricted to a compact subset of $w>\\gamma>0$, as holds for the bins under consideration. For fixed sufficiently small $b>0$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\int_b^{1/2}D_\\alpha(1-\\alpha)\\frac{d\\alpha}{\\alpha}\n     &=D_b(1)-1,\\label{ex:density-integral}\\\\\n D_b(1)&\\le\\frac{e^{-\\gamma_E}}b(1+O(e^{-c/b}))\n       \\label{ex:density-upper}\n\\end{aligned}$$ with an absolute $c>0$.       "exact_statement": "For sufficiently large $x$, the denominators in <a href=\"#ex:proxy-definition\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ex:proxy-definition\">[ex:proxy-definition]</a> are positive and $0\\le c_I\\le1$. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
```
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Omega and omega** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Let $\Omega(n)$ be the number of prime factors of $n$, counted with multiplicity, and let $\omega(n)$ count its distinct prime factors.
- **least prime factor and divisor function** ([source](#sec:preliminaries)): We put $P^-(1)=\infty$, where $P^-(n)$ is the least prime factor of $n>1$, and write $\tau(n)$ for its divisor function.
- **dyadic comparability** ([source](#sec:preliminaries)): The notation $n\asymp Y$ restricts $n$ to a fixed constant enlargement of a dyad $[Y,2Y)$.
- **characters extended by zero** ([source](#sec:preliminaries)): Dirichlet characters are extended by zero on nonunits.
- **Dirichlet L-function notation** ([source](#lp:L-tail)): We write $D(s,\chi)$ for the Dirichlet $L$-function, to distinguish it from $L=\log x$.
- **big group** ([source](#pm-h-9)): Each big group is the set of all primes in an interval.
- **invariant endpoint core** ([source](#sh:core)): The word invariant refers to the identity $F(vn)=F(n)$ whenever $v_*=1$.
- **prime-extraction quantities** ([source](#ex:mass-reminder)): Put $N_u=2u+1$ and $a_x(u)=A(u)\Psi(u/x)$, so that $a_x(u)\ge0$ and $X_A=\sum_u a_x(u)$.
- **micro and macro bands** ([source](#ex:micro-choice)): Call $Q_j$ with $j\ge j'$ the micro bands and those with $j<j'$ the macro bands.
- **assignment** ([source](#stmt-ex-mask)): An assignment $a$ consists of the complete ordered $r_0$-tuples in all the micro bands and one prime mark from each $\mathcal P_g$.

```json
      "exact_definition": "An assignment $a$ consists of the complete ordered $r_0$-tuples in all the micro bands and one prime mark from each $\\mathcal P_g$.",
      "interpretation": "A choice of full micro-band tuples and one mark per group prime set, used to build the positive majorant.",
      "kind": "definition",
      "name": "assignment",
      "scope": "Lemma on assignment and mask majorant"
    }
  ],
  "generation_status": "COMPLETE",
  "pm_id": "PM-011.3",
  "schema": "pm.objects.v2"
}
```

|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-scale-L | L | = | $\log x$ | Main parameter |
| q-scale-W | W | = | $\exp(\sqrt L)$ | Roughness threshold |
| q-radius-r-star | r_* | = | $T^4/L$ | Local radius |
| q-prime-poly-saving | Prime Dirichlet polynomial saving | ≤ | $L^{-A}$ | Implied constant depends on the fixed parameters |
| q-power-sum-mean-value | Power-sum mean value | ≤ | $\exp(C_1k^{C_2})M^{2s-K+1/100}$ | Absolute constants |
| q-correlation-saving | Twisted correlation sum | ≤ | $L^{-D_0}$ | Implied constant depends on fixed data |
| q-harmonic-band-mass | Band harmonic mass | = | $\log\frac65+o(1)$ | As x tends to infinity |
| q-group-tail | Group tail mass | ≤ | $L^C\exp(-\sigma L^{3/5})$ | Sufficiently large x |
| q-candidate-mass | Candidate mass lower bound | ≥ | $xL^{-C_A}$ | Constants depend on fixed geometry and the cutoff |
| q-squarefree-loss | Nonsquarefree predecessor mass | = | $o(X_A/L)$ | As x tends to infinity |
| q-type-ii-saving | Type II bilinear sum | ≤ | $xL^{-D_*}$ | Implied constant depends on fixed data |
| q-congruence-remainder | Sum of remainders over moduli | ≤ | $xL^{-D}+X_A L^{-18}$ | No effective threshold for x |
| q-box-constant | Dyadic box pair weight | ≤ | $C_{\mathrm{box}}X_AV(W)^2$ | Constant depends on weight data and micro-band choices |
| q-surviving-mass | Surviving mass after unbalanced removal | ≥ | $\frac{\mathfrak S X_A}{2L}$ | Sufficiently large x, small b_1 |
| q-balanced-composites | Balanced composite weight | ≤ | $C_{\mathrm{bal}}(\kappa+1/L)\frac{X_A}{L}+o(X_A/L)$ | Constant fixed before the balanced width |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-classical-inputs** [claim](#stmt-pre-primes) / [proof](#stmt-pre-primes): Record classical prime estimates in the forms used later. -> Prime estimates
- **step-moments** [claim](#stmt-pre-moments) / [proof](#stmt-pre-moments): Record large sieve and moment bounds. -> Moment bounds
- **step-derivatives** [claim](#stmt-pre-derivatives) / [proof](#stmt-pre-derivatives): Record derivative tests for exponential sums. -> Derivative tests
- **step-separation** [claim](#stmt-pre-separation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pre-separation): Integrate by parts in Fourier inversion and truncate frequencies. -> Separated phases
- **step-mean-value** [claim](#stmt-lp-mean-value) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lp-mean-value): Iterate a p-adic recurrence for power-sum solution counts. -> Degree-uniform mean-value bound
- **step-log-phase** [claim](#stmt-lp-log-phase) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lp-log-phase): Shift averaging, Taylor expansion, box occupancy and Holder with the mean-value bound. -> Phase cancellation on progressions
- **step-l-upper** [claim](#stmt-lp-l-upper) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lp-l-upper): Approximate the L-function by a truncated sum and use phase cancellation on dyads. -> Upper bound near the line
- **step-local-log-derivative** [claim](#stmt-lp-local-log-derivative) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lp-local-log-derivative): Jensen formula, Blaschke quotient, Harnack inequality. -> Local zero-sum formula
- **step-zero-free** [claim](#stmt-lp-zero-free) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lp-zero-free): Trigonometric positivity with the local formula at two heights. -> Zero-free strip
- **step-prime-polynomial** [claim](#stmt-lp-prime-polynomial) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lp-zero-free-2): Smooth the interval, Mellin inversion, shift the contour into the strip, remove prime powers and the log weight. -> Long prime polynomial bound
- **step-transference** [claim](#stmt-sh-transference) / [proof](#stmt-sh-transference): Imported local transference from the companion paper. -> Small physical pairing
- **step-ideal** [claim](#stmt-sh-ideal) / [proof](#stmt-sh-ideal): Imported residual ideal family and comparison kernels. -> Ideal family with small norm
- **step-mellin-energy** [claim](#stmt-sh-mellin-energy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sh-mellin-energy): Plancherel with a bump kernel, kernel replacement and Fourier inversion. -> Energy to Mellin polynomial inequality
- **step-correlation** [claim](#stmt-sh-correlation) / [proof](#stmt-sh-correlation): Residual pairing by transference, cancellation of the shared sign, comparison terms by minor and major arcs, exceptional times by a sparse mean-square argument. -> Twisted correlation bound
- **step-group-tail** [claim](#stmt-wt-tail) / [proof](#proof-stmt-wt-tail): Insert a small power weight and bound the group Euler product. -> Group tail bound
- **step-candidate-mass** [claim](#stmt-wt-mass) / [proof](#proof-stmt-wt-mass): Probability identity, upper bound from a free slot, lower bound by midpoint localization. -> Two-sided mass
- **step-squarefree-loss** [claim](#stmt-wt-squarefree) / [proof](#proof-stmt-wt-squarefree): Bound probabilities of repeated primes in bands and groups. -> Negligible nonsquarefree mass
- **step-type-ii** [claim](#stmt-ti-distribution) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ti-distribution): Discard large group part, smooth allocation, Fourier separation, Cauchy-Schwarz, determinant change of variables, apply the twisted correlation. -> Type II distribution
- **step-congruence** [claim](#stmt-cg-distribution) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cg-distribution): Character orthogonality, large sieve for large conductors, Siegel-Walfisz for small conductors. -> Congruence distribution
- **step-block-sieve** [claim](#stmt-ex-block-sieve) / [proof](#stmt-ex-block-sieve): Imported weighted block sieve. -> Sieve formula
- **step-proxies** [claim](#stmt-ex-proxies) / [proof](#stmt-ex-proxies): Imported local-density proxy estimates. -> Proxy discrepancy and density bounds
- **step-mask-majorant** [claim](#stmt-ex-mask) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ex-mask): Dominate the weight by assignments with random bans. -> Positive majorant
- **step-dyadic-box** [claim](#stmt-ex-box) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ex-box): Apply the fixed-depth block sieve per assignment and sum. -> Box bound
- **step-replacement** [claim](#stmt-ex-replacement) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ex-replacement): Apply the Type II theorem to the difference of test and proxy. -> Proxy replacement
- **step-presieve** [claim](#stmt-ex-presieve) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ex-presieve): Block sieve with the congruence remainder bound. -> Initial sifted mass
- **step-conditional-sieve** [claim](#stmt-ex-conditional) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ex-conditional): Block sieve with growing depth over each prime in a bin. -> Conditional main term
- **step-unbalanced** [claim](#stmt-ex-unbalanced) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ex-unbalanced): Replace roughness by proxies and subtract from the presieved mass. -> Surviving mass
- **step-balanced** [claim](#stmt-ex-balanced) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ex-balanced): Replace primality by proxies and apply the box bound. -> Balanced composite bound
- **step-main-theorem** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ex-balanced-2): Combine surviving mass and balanced bound, remove nonsquarefree loss, read off parity. -> Infinitely many primes with the stated predecessor

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-main-parity**: Infinitely many primes p exist for which p-1 is squarefree and has an even number of prime factors counted with multiplicity.
- **inv-ineffective-threshold**: The argument gives no effective threshold for x, so no explicit prime of the required kind is located by it.
- **inv-prime-polynomial-range**: The long prime polynomial estimate is stated only for fixed exponents with 0<tau<eta<1 and fixed C and A, and for large enough frequencies.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-explicit-prime-generator**: Use the main theorem as a procedure that outputs primes p with p-1 squarefree and of even prime-factor count. -> The proof is a positive-mass argument for all large x with no effective threshold, so it yields no search bound and no certified output.
- **anti-small-height-polynomial**: Apply the long prime polynomial cancellation at small heights t. -> The cancellation is only proved when the height exceeds a logarithmic power, so the saving may fail below that threshold.
- **anti-effective-siegel-walfisz**: Use the Siegel-Walfisz estimate quoted here to get explicit numerical error bounds. -> The constants may be ineffective, so no numerical bound follows.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The results are asymptotic existence and estimate statements for x tending to infinity with ineffective thresholds and constants, and the source states that no effective threshold for x is asserted. No finite procedure with checkable input and output is given, so no executable reference or independent oracle is meaningful.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper proves an existence theorem about primes and does not state a program or library. Its inputs are imported analytic estimates, and the text declines to reprove them, so there is nothing to implement. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No stochastic process, dynamics or model is simulated. The random draws in the weight construction are bookkeeping devices for harmonic measures inside a proof. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper gives no checker or tool-style procedure with a decidable output. Its parity-selecting weight is an analytic device whose thresholds are not effective. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The main result is non-constructive and gives no explicit algorithm. Thresholds in $x$ are not effective and the classical inputs may have ineffective constants, so no running time or output guarantee can be extracted. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No representation or data structure with operations is proposed. The prime groups and marked lists are proof-internal combinatorial objects. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No kernel, circuit or numerical computation is described, only asymptotic estimates for exponential and character sums. The estimates carry no explicit constants that hardware could exploit. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result concerns the parity of the number of prime factors of p-1, but the paper states no key-generation or hardness claim. The proof establishes infinitude without any effective way to produce or recognise such primes. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No learning problem, data distribution or loss appears. The only analysis is of prime sums with explicit sieve weights. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper fixes parameters in a dependency order so that the estimates close, but poses no objective or feasibility problem to optimise. The parameter choices are existential constraints. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No protocol, communication or concurrency model is present. Nothing in the weighted dilation graphs corresponds to a networked system. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The nearest outlet is conceptual: a mathematical record of how common-sign cancellation can bypass the sieve parity obstruction. That is an analytic observation, not an engineering construction. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-ineffective-threshold** (nonconstructive): The main theorem is proved for x tending to infinity with no explicit threshold, so it cannot be used to locate a specific qualifying prime. Blocked use: ['Using the theorem as a generator of primes p with p-1 squarefree and of even prime-factor count', 'Claiming a numerical bound for the first such prime']
- **limit-siegel-walfisz-constants** (approximation): The Siegel–Walfisz input holds only for moduli of fixed logarithmic-power size and may have ineffective constants. Blocked use: ['Deriving explicit numerical error bounds from the prime estimates', 'Applying the estimate to moduli above a fixed power of the logarithm']
- **limit-prime-polynomial-height** (hypothesis): The long prime polynomial cancellation is stated only for heights between a large logarithmic power and x^2, with fixed exponents and a modulus of logarithmic-power size. Blocked use: ['Using the cancellation at small heights t', 'Using it for heights beyond x^2']
- **limit-fixed-dimension-separation** (formal_scope): Smooth separation requires a fixed dimension of the box and a fixed exponent for the frequency truncation chosen before the accuracy. Blocked use: ['Separating variables of a box whose dimension grows with x without extra argument']
- **limit-discrepancy-hypothesis** (hypothesis): The twisted correlation and Type II bounds need a small-discrepancy hypothesis on the first sequence only; the exponent B depends on the fixed data and the saving wanted. Blocked use: ['Applying the correlation theorem to a first sequence without the discrepancy bound', 'Choosing B before the saving exponent is known']
- **limit-short-intervals** (edge_case): Prime asymptotics are used only in fixed positive-power bands or relative lengths a fixed negative power of L, never on arbitrarily short intervals. Blocked use: ['Assuming a relative prime asymptotic on every short interval']
- **limit-imported-results** (formal_scope): Transference, ideal-kernel, block-sieve and proxy results are imported from a companion paper and are not reproved here. Blocked use: ['Treating the imported results as proved within this paper']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove that there are infinitely many primes <i>p</i> for which $`p-1`$ is squarefree and has an even number of prime factors. Equivalently, there are infinitely many primes <i>p</i> with $`\mu(p-1)=1`$.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

Let $\Omega(n)$ be the number of prime factors of $n$, counted with multiplicity, and let $\omega(n)$ count its distinct prime factors. We prove the following affirmative answer to the parity question for predecessors of primes.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *There are infinitely many primes $p$ such that $p-1$ is squarefree and $\Omega(p-1)$ is even. In particular, there are infinitely many such primes under either convention for counting prime factors.*

</div>

The squarefreeness condition is useful rather than cosmetic: it removes the ambiguity between the two counting conventions. Writing $p=2u+1$, we will construct nonnegative weights on odd integers $u$ for which $\Omega(u)$ is odd. These weights have enough bilinear distribution to detect prime values of $2u+1$, and their nonsquarefree part is negligible at the scale of the detected prime mass.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
###### Relation to earlier work.

Prime-factor parity is a classical obstruction in sieve theory: congruence information alone need not distinguish the two Liouville signs. The role of additional bilinear information is illustrated by the asymptotic sieve of Friedlander and Iwaniec <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[FriedlanderIwaniec](#ref-FriedlanderIwaniec), Section 1]. Work on smooth shifted primes, including Baker and Harman <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[BakerHarman](#ref-BakerHarman)] and Lichtman <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Lichtman](#ref-Lichtman)], provides related context for imposing multiplicative restrictions on a prime’s predecessor. The parity condition here is a different restriction; we do not deduce it from smoothness alone.

Our structural inputs are the dilation-graph transference, ideal-kernel, weighted-sieve, and proxy estimates of the companion paper *Weighted dilation graphs, smooth shifted primes and totient fibers <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[SmoothShifted](#ref-SmoothShifted)]* , hereafter S. Their hypotheses and the conclusions needed here are stated where they enter the proof. We do not reprove those results.

S’s shifted-correlation argument uses a rough integer in a designated long factor slot and unsigned marked weights. Neither smoothness of the predecessor nor that unsigned statement gives the parity conclusion of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>. The new work is to permit a prime in the long slot and the same group-supported Liouville sign at both endpoints. We prove both extensions here.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
###### The two analytic extensions.

First, a logarithmic-phase estimate gives arbitrary fixed logarithmic cancellation in a prime Dirichlet polynomial of positive-power length at all sufficiently large logarithmic heights up to $x^2$. A quantitative power-sum iteration supplies the weak high-height zero-free strip needed for this estimate. The growing degree and all its constants are kept explicit enough for the application.

Second, the sign of a shared dilation occurs twice in a lifted correlation and cancels by complete multiplicativity. This observation allows the graph reduction to retain a parity-sensitive weight. The comparison terms are controlled by local Fourier energy: a small-prime factor restricts difficult frequencies to a sparse set, the new prime estimate treats its large frequencies, and a prescribed character–Mellin discrepancy treats its small frequencies. The resulting correlation estimate implies a Type II theorem for the nonnegative parity-selecting weight.

The common-sign cancellation and the prime-slot estimate are stated separately, so their use is not tied to the final sieve. In the extraction argument, ordinary congruence distribution handles the initial sieve; the stronger Type II statement justifies replacing prime and roughness indicators by bounded local-density proxies. An upper bound for balanced semiprimes then leaves positive prime mass.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
###### Organization.

Section <a href="#sec:preliminaries" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:preliminaries">2</a> fixes conventions and records the classical inputs. Section <a href="#lp:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lp:section">3</a> proves the prime-polynomial estimate, Lemma <a href="#lp:prime-polynomial" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lp:prime-polynomial">6</a>, and Section <a href="#sh:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sh:section">4</a> proves the twisted correlation, Theorem <a href="#sh:correlation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sh:correlation">12</a>. Section <a href="#wt:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="wt:section">5</a> constructs the weights. Their Type II and congruence distribution are Theorems <a href="#ti:distribution" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ti:distribution">20</a> and <a href="#cg:distribution" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cg:distribution">22</a>, proved in Sections <a href="#ti:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ti:section">6</a> and <a href="#cg:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cg:section">7</a>. Section <a href="#ex:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ex:section">8</a> extracts the required primes. All auxiliary constants are fixed before $x$ tends to infinity; in particular, the final proxy precision is chosen only after the required discrepancy exponent. We make this order explicit at the steps where it matters.

<a id="sec:preliminaries"></a>
### Conventions and classical estimates

Throughout the proof, $$x\longrightarrow\infty,\qquad L=\log x,\qquad T=\log L,
 \qquad W=\exp(\sqrt L),\qquad \mathrm{e}(t)=\exp(2\pi i t).$$ A logarithmic power always has a fixed exponent, chosen before $x$ tends to infinity. The implied constants may depend on all previously fixed parameters. No effective threshold for $x$ is asserted. The letters $p$ and $q$ in explicitly indicated prime sums range over primes; general factorization variables are positive integers. We put $P^-(1)=\infty$, where $P^-(n)$ is the least prime factor of $n>1$, and write $\tau(n)$ for its divisor function. The notation $n\asymp Y$ restricts $n$ to a fixed constant enlargement of a dyad $[Y,2Y)$. All such enlargements remain bounded independently of $x$. Dirichlet characters are extended by zero on nonunits.

We use the following classical estimates in the forms recorded in S . This explicit list fixes their uniformity; it does not require any distribution theorem beyond the stated ranges.

<a id="stmt-pre-primes"></a>
<div id="pre:primes" class="proposition">

**Proposition 2** (Prime estimates). *The prime number theorem holds with an error smaller than every fixed negative logarithmic power. Mertens’ estimates give $$\sum_{p\le y}\frac1p=\log\log y+O(1),\qquad
 \prod_{p\le y}\left(1-\frac1p\right)
 \sim\frac{e^{-\gamma_E}}{\log y},$$ where $\gamma_E$ is Euler’s constant. For fixed $A,C>0$, uniformly for $r\le(\log y)^C$ and $(a,r)=1$, the Siegel–Walfisz estimate is $$\#\{p\le y:p\equiv a\pmod r\}
 =\frac{\mathop{\mathrm{Li}}(y)}{\varphi(r)}
   +O_{A,C}\left(\frac{y}{(\log y)^A}\right).$$ Its constants need not be effective.*

</div>

All short intervals used with the prime number theorem have either a fixed logarithmic-length interpretation in a fixed positive-power band, or relative length a fixed negative power of $L$; the absolute error above is made sufficiently small before taking their differences. We never assume a relative prime asymptotic on every arbitrarily short interval.

<a id="stmt-pre-moments"></a>
<div id="pre:moments" class="proposition">

**Proposition 3** (Large sieve and moment bounds). *For arbitrary coefficients supported on the integers of an interval of length $H$, $$\sum_{r\le R}\frac r{\varphi(r)}
 \sum_{\chi\bmod r}^{*}
 \left|\sum_n a_n\chi(n)\right|^2
 \ll (H+1+R^2)\sum_n|a_n|^2.$$ The asterisk denotes primitive characters. For every fixed integer $k\ge0$ there is $C_k$ such that $$\sum_{n\le Y}\tau(n)^k\ll_k Y(\log(2Y))^{C_k},\qquad
 \sum_{n\le Y}\frac{\tau(n)^k}{n}
 \ll_k(\log(2Y))^{C_k}.$$ If $P(t)=\sum_{n\le Y}c_n n^{it}$, then on any real interval $I$ of length $H$, $$\int_I|P(t)|^2\,dt
 \ll (H+Y\log(2Y))\sum_n|c_n|^2.$$ For a set $\mathcal T\subset I$ with mutual spacing at least one, $$\sum_{t\in\mathcal T}|P(t)|^2
 \ll (H+1+Y\log(2Y))(\log(2Y))^2\sum_n|c_n|^2.$$ The constants in the last two estimates are absolute, independently of how the coefficients were formed.*

</div>

These are Theorem 2.3 and Lemmas 2.5–2.6 of S. In particular, the fixed-moment estimate applies to a convolution of a fixed number of logarithmically bounded sequences, giving coefficient-square mass $UL^{O(1)}$ on a dyad of size $U$. With reciprocal-index normalization the bound is $L^{O(1)}/U$. For the one growing power used later, we instead prove a factorial coefficient bound and use the coefficient-independent mean-square inequality.

<a id="stmt-pre-derivatives"></a>
<div id="pre:derivatives" class="proposition">

**Proposition 4** (Derivative tests). *Let $f$ be real-valued on an interval containing $H$ consecutive integers. If $f'$ is monotone and remains in $[j+\lambda,j+1-\lambda]$ for an integer $j$ and $0<\lambda\le1/2$, then $$\sum_n\mathrm{e}(f(n))\ll\lambda^{-1}.$$ If instead $f$ is twice continuously differentiable and $\lambda\le|f''|\le C\lambda$ throughout the interval, then $$\sum_n\mathrm{e}(f(n))\ll_C H\sqrt\lambda+\lambda^{-1/2}.$$ Both estimates apply on every subinterval satisfying their hypotheses.*

</div>

This is the form of S, Lemma 2.7. The distance from integers in the first test is part of the hypothesis, not merely a lower bound on the absolute derivative.

We will repeatedly separate smooth functions in logarithmic coordinates. The following elementary version of S, Lemma 2.8, explains the order in which frequency cutoffs and accuracies may be chosen.

<a id="stmt-pre-separation"></a>
<div id="pre:separation" class="lemma">

**Lemma 5** (Smooth separation). *Let $F$ be smooth and supported on a fixed bounded box in $\mathbb{R}^b$, with $b$ fixed. Suppose all derivatives of order $j$ are bounded by $C_jL^{C(j+1)}$. Fourier inversion separates its variables with integrated absolute coefficient mass $L^{O(1)}$. There is a fixed exponent $C'>0$, depending on $b,C$, such that truncating each frequency at $L^{C'}$ makes an error $O_A(L^{-A})$ for every fixed $A>0$. The exponent $C'$ is fixed before $A$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-pre-separation"><summary>Proof of stmt-pre-separation</summary>

*Proof.* Integration by parts gives, for each fixed $j$, an integrable tail bounded by a constant times $L^{C(j+1)}(1+|\xi|)^{-j}$. Using a fixed $j>b$ first bounds the full integral by a fixed logarithmic power. Outside $|\xi|\ge L^{C'}$ the same calculation gives $$O_j\bigl(L^{C(j+1)-C'(j-b)}\bigr).$$ Fix $C'>C$ and then increase $j$ for any prescribed $A$. The Fourier phase factors into one-dimensional phases. Applied to normalized logarithmic coordinates, these are multiplicative power twists. ◻

</details>

The fixed-dimension condition in Lemma <a href="#pre:separation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pre:separation">5</a> will not be used with a growing-dimensional box. In the allocation argument there are $O(T)$ separate one-dimensional transitions, each with a fixed Fourier integral norm. Their product costs only $\exp(O(T))=L^{O(1)}$; a telescoping estimate controls their combined tails.

The weighted block sieve and local-density proxies are stated in the prime-extraction section, where their particular coefficient bounds and precision dependence are needed. The generic dilation-graph results are likewise stated at their application in the shifted correlation section. These are the only nonclassical imported ingredients of the proof.

<a id="lp:section"></a>
### A long prime polynomial

The correlation argument will require cancellation in a prime polynomial at frequencies as large as $x^2$. We prove the required estimate here, including the dependence on a growing degree in the elementary mean-value argument. Throughout this section, $L=\log x$ and $T=\log L$.

<a id="stmt-lp-prime-polynomial"></a>
<div id="lp:prime-polynomial" class="lemma">

<a id="lp:prime-estimate"></a>
**Lemma 6** (Long prime polynomial). *Fix $0<\tau<\eta<1$, $C>0$, and $A>0$. There is a constant $B_0=B_0(\tau,\eta,C,A)$ such that, uniformly for $$\frac{x^\tau}{2}\le N\le x^\eta,\qquad
 q\le L^C,\qquad L^{B_0}\le |t|\le x^2,$$ every Dirichlet character $\chi$ modulo $q$ and every interval $I\subset[N,2N]$ satisfy $$\label{lp:prime-estimate}
 \left|\sum_{p\in I}\chi(p)p^{-1+it}\right|
 \ll_{\tau,\eta,C,A} L^{-A}.$$*

</div>

The proof proceeds from a quantitative power-sum estimate to cancellation for a logarithmic phase, then to a high-height zero-free strip, and finally to primes by Mellin inversion. We first establish a power-sum estimate with sufficient uniformity in its degree. For integers $k\ge2$, $s\ge1$, and $M\ge1$, let $J_{s,k}(M)$ count the solutions of $$\sum_{i=1}^s u_i^j=\sum_{i=1}^s v_i^j
 \quad(1\le j\le k),\qquad 1\le u_i,v_i\le M.$$ Writing $K=k(k+1)/2$ and $$f(\boldsymbol\alpha)=\sum_{n=1}^M
       \mathrm{e}\!\left(\sum_{j=1}^k\alpha_j n^j\right),$$ orthogonality gives $J_{s,k}(M)=\int_{[0,1]^k}|f(\boldsymbol\alpha)|^{2s}
\,d\boldsymbol\alpha$.

The argument uses the classical Vinogradov mean-value method in Linnik’s $p$-adic form; see Wooley <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[Wooley2012](#ref-Wooley2012), Section 2, pp. 1583–1585] for an exposition. We derive the required degree-uniform quantitative estimate below, without invoking the modern efficient-congruencing theorem of that paper.

<a id="stmt-lp-mean-value"></a>
<div id="lp:mean-value" class="lemma">

<a id="lp:mean-value-bound"></a>
**Lemma 7** (A quantitative power-sum bound). *There are absolute constants $C_1,C_2>0$ such that, for every integer $k\ge2$, some integer $s$ with $k\le s\le C_1k^4$ satisfies $$\label{lp:mean-value-bound}
 J_{s,k}(M)\le \exp(C_1k^{C_2})M^{2s-K+1/100}
 \qquad(M\ge1).$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lp-mean-value"><summary>Proof of stmt-lp-mean-value</summary>

<a id="lp:inductive-bound"></a>
*Proof.* We iterate estimates $$\label{lp:inductive-bound}
